11 October 2026 — Li's coefficients for finite configurations: an off-line pair at height \(\gamma\) and distance \(\delta\) lowers the \(n\)-th coefficient by at most about one unit while \(n\le\gamma^2/\delta\), and makes infinitely many coefficients negative beyond

Working proof of an elementary statement about finite configurations; the lead's sources are not read and it stays open. Li threshold. Produced by Claude Code (claude-opus-5-5, low effort).

Chosen. This entry takes the route review's third lead, Li's criterion, ahead of the queue's head: the first two leads have each had two consecutive entries (the queue tool's status at the starts of those cycles), this lead has had none (its status at this cycle's start), and it is the remaining queued lead that is a positivity statement about a sequence, bearing on off-line exclusion. At the goal's parameters it could give a positivity that an off-line zero at height \(T\) violates at an index \(n\) where something is known about the coefficients; if the index needed grows like \(T^2\) with nothing known there, it gives nothing.

Question. The missing implication is an estimate excluding one off-line zero. For a finite configuration closed under the two symmetries, with coefficients \(\lambda_n=\sum_\rho m_\rho(1-(1-1/\rho)^n)\): at which \(n\) can an off-line pair make \(\lambda_n\) negative, by how much does it lower \(\lambda_n\) before that, and can pairs cancel? The test ends with a proved threshold.

New mathematics. One elementary proposition. With \(\tau_\rho=\delta_\rho/(\min(\beta_\rho,1-\beta_\rho)^2+\gamma_\rho^2)\) for an off-line point at distance \(\delta_\rho\) from the line: \(\lambda_n\ge S_{\mathrm{on}}(n)-4\sum_{\mathrm{off}}m_\rho\sinh^2(n\tau_\rho/2)\), where \(S_{\mathrm{on}}(n)\ge0\) is the on-line points' contribution; in particular for \(n\le1/\tau_{\max}\) the off-line pairs lower \(\lambda_n\) by less than \(1.09\) per pair; and if there is any off-line point, \(\lambda_n\to-\infty\) along infinitely many \(n\). The last part is the finite model of the criterion's hard direction, which I believe is known in general (Bombieri and Lagarias, unread); no novelty is claimed.

Goal progress. Unchanged. In the model, positivity of all coefficients is equivalent to all points on the line, with no cancellation among pairs in the limit, but a negative coefficient at index \(n\) needs \(n\tau_{\max}>\log(S_{\mathrm{on}}(n)/M_{\mathrm{off}})\), \(\tau_{\max}\) being the largest \(\delta/\gamma^2\) of the configuration. So, provided the on-line contribution \(S_{\mathrm{on}}(n)\) exceeds \(M_{\mathrm{off}}e^{n\tau_{\max}}\) on the range, which is assumed and not derived for \(\zeta\), positivity of \(\lambda_n\) for \(n\le N\) constrains only configurations with some pair of \(\gamma^2/\delta\lesssim N\). Without that proviso the statement is false: a quadruple with no on-line points has a negative coefficient already at \(n\) of order \(\gamma\). For a zero at height \(T\) the range is \(n\gtrsim T^2\). That nothing is known about the coefficients there short of the zeros' location is an untested hypothesis of mine; the reviewer recalls, unread, a theorem that a one-sided subexponential lower bound for the coefficients is already equivalent to the goal, which would make any such bound a restatement. Bearing. At the goal's parameters this gives nothing: it locates the index range in which the criterion would have to be verified and supplies no means of verifying it. The cycle was spent on it because the lead proposes positivity of a sequence, a statement of another kind than the forms examined, and its first test had not been made.

General statement. prop:li-coefficient-threshold, for every finite multiset of complex points other than \(0\) and \(1\) that is closed, with multiplicities, under \(\rho\mapsto\bar\rho\) and \(\rho\mapsto1-\bar\rho\), and every \(n\ge1\).

Statement and proof

Let \(R\) be such a multiset, \(\rho=\beta+i\gamma\), \(w_\rho=1-1/\rho=(\rho-1)/\rho\), and \(\lambda_n=\sum_{\rho\in R}m_\rho(1-w_\rho^n)\). A point is on-line if \(\beta=1/2\). For an off-line point put \(\delta_\rho=|\beta-1/2|\), \(r_\rho=\max(|w_\rho|,|w_\rho|^{-1})>1\) and \(\tau_\rho=\delta_\rho/(\min(\beta,1-\beta)^2+\gamma^2)\). Sums run over distinct points with their multiplicities. The map \(\rho\mapsto1-\bar\rho\) is an involution whose fixed points are the on-line points, so the off-line points fall into partner pairs \(\{\rho,1-\bar\rho\}\) of equal multiplicity; a sum over off-line pairs takes one point from each, and a quadruple \(\rho,\bar\rho,1-\rho,1-\bar\rho\) is two pairs.

Proposition (Li coefficient threshold). prop:li-coefficient-threshold.

  1. \(\lambda_n\) is real and \[\lambda_n=S_{\mathrm{on}}(n)+\sum_{\text{off-line pairs}}m_\rho\bigl(2-(r_\rho^{\,n}+r_\rho^{-n})\cos n\varphi_\rho\bigr),\qquad S_{\mathrm{on}}(n)=\sum_{\text{on-line}}m_\rho(1-\cos n\theta_\rho)\ \ge0,\] where \(w_\rho=e^{i\theta_\rho}\) on the line and \(w_\rho=|w_\rho|e^{i\varphi_\rho}\) off it.
  2. \(\log r_\rho\le\tau_\rho\), and \[\lambda_n\ \ge\ S_{\mathrm{on}}(n)-4\sum_{\text{off-line pairs}}m_\rho\sinh^2(n\tau_\rho/2).\] In particular, for \(n\le1/\max_\rho\tau_\rho\), \(\lambda_n\ge S_{\mathrm{on}}(n)-4\sinh^2(\tfrac12)M_{\mathrm{off}}\), with \(M_{\mathrm{off}}\) the number of off-line pairs with multiplicity and \(4\sinh^2(\tfrac12)<1.09\); and if \(\lambda_n<0\) then \(M_{\mathrm{off}}\ge1\) and \(n\max_\rho\tau_\rho>\log(S_{\mathrm{on}}(n)/M_{\mathrm{off}})\), the logarithm of \(0\) read as \(-\infty\).
  3. If \(R\) has an off-line point, then \(\lambda_n<0\) for infinitely many \(n\), and \(\lambda_n\to-\infty\) along a sequence. If it has none, \(\lambda_n=S_{\mathrm{on}}(n)\ge0\) for all \(n\).

Proof. (1) \(|\rho-1|^2-|\rho|^2=1-2\beta\), so \(|w_\rho|=1\) exactly on the line. For the partner \(\rho'=1-\bar\rho\), \(w_{\rho'}=-\bar\rho/(1-\bar\rho)=\bar\rho/(\bar\rho-1)=1/\overline{w_\rho}\), which has modulus \(|w_\rho|^{-1}\) and the same argument \(\varphi_\rho\). So an off-line pair contributes \(m_\rho(2-(r^n+r^{-n})e^{in\varphi_\rho})\), and an on-line point, its own partner, \(m_\rho(1-e^{in\theta_\rho})\). Closure under conjugation, which sends \(w\) to \(\bar w\), makes the sum real, so it equals the sum of the real parts. (2) Take the point of the pair with \(\beta>1/2\); then \(r^2=|\rho|^2/|\rho-1|^2=1+(2\beta-1)/((1-\beta)^2+\gamma^2)\) and \(\log r\le\tfrac12(2\beta-1)/((1-\beta)^2+\gamma^2)=\tau_\rho\), the minimum in \(\tau_\rho\) being \(1-\beta\). Since \(\cos\le1\), \(2-(r^n+r^{-n})\cos n\varphi\ge2-r^n-r^{-n}=-(r^{n/2}-r^{-n/2})^2=-4\sinh^2(\tfrac n2\log r)\ge-4\sinh^2(n\tau_\rho/2)\). For \(n\tau_\rho\le1\) each term is at least \(-4\sinh^2(1/2)\). If \(\lambda_n<0\), then \(S_{\mathrm{on}}(n)<4M_{\mathrm{off}}\sinh^2(n\tau_{\max}/2)<M_{\mathrm{off}}e^{n\tau_{\max}}\), using \(4\sinh^2(x/2)<e^{x}\). (3) Let \(r_*>1\) be the largest \(r_\rho\) and \(P\) the set of off-line pairs attaining it, with total multiplicity \(M_*\). By the pigeonhole principle applied to the points \((n\varphi_\rho/2\pi)_{\rho\in P}\) of a torus (Dirichlet's simultaneous approximation; if all the \(\varphi_\rho/2\pi\) are rational, take multiples of a common denominator) there are infinitely many \(n\) with \(n\varphi_\rho\) within \(\pi/3\) of a multiple of \(2\pi\) for every pair in \(P\); for these, \(\sum_Pm_\rho(r_*^n+r_*^{-n})\cos n\varphi_\rho\ge\tfrac12M_*r_*^n\). All other terms of (1) are bounded in absolute value by \(2|R|+2|R|r_2^{\,n}\), where \(|R|\) counts with multiplicity and \(r_2<r_*\) is the next largest modulus, or \(1\). Hence \(\lambda_n\le2|R|(1+r_2^{\,n})+2M_*-\tfrac12M_*r_*^n\to-\infty\) along these \(n\). The last sentence is (1). \(\square\)

Degenerate cases. The empty configuration has \(\lambda_n=0\). A configuration of one off-line pair and its conjugates, with no on-line points, has \(S_{\mathrm{on}}=0\) and (2) gives no positivity: indeed \(\lambda_n<0\) as soon as \(\cos n\varphi\) is close enough to \(1\), by an amount at most \(4m\sinh^2(n\tau/2)\) per pair. Real off-line points (\(\gamma=0\), \(0<\beta<1\)) are allowed: there \(w<0\), \(\varphi=\pi\), and the pair's term is negative for every even \(n\). Real points outside \([0,1]\) are allowed by the statement, with \(\varphi=0\), and their pair's term is negative for every \(n\ge1\). The points \(0\) and \(1\) are excluded: at \(0\), \(w\) is undefined, and at \(1\), \(w=0\) and the partner \(0\) has none. When \(S_{\mathrm{on}}(n)\le M_{\mathrm{off}}\) the last inequality of (2) is vacuous. Closure under conjugation is used only for realness: parts 2 and 3 hold for \(\Re\lambda_n\) without it.

What it says

The index at which a pair matters. For a pair at height \(\gamma\) and distance \(\delta\) from the line, \(\tau\le\delta/\gamma^2\). Below \(n=\gamma^2/\delta\) the pair lowers \(\lambda_n\) by less than \(1.09m\) below what it would be without the pair's term (compared with the same pair moved onto the line, whose two points contribute between \(0\) and \(4m\), the deficit can be up to \(5.09m\)). For \(\lambda_n\) to be negative the index must exceed \(\tau_{\max}^{-1}\log(S_{\mathrm{on}}(n)/M_{\mathrm{off}})\), with \(\tau_{\max}^{-1}\) at least the smallest \(\gamma^2/\delta\) among the pairs; this says something only when \(S_{\mathrm{on}}(n)>M_{\mathrm{off}}\). So positivity of the coefficients up to \(N\) says nothing about a configuration all of whose pairs have \(\gamma^2/\delta\) well above \(N\), whenever the on-line contribution at those indices exceeds the number of off-line pairs. How large \(S_{\mathrm{on}}(n)\) is for the zeros of \(\zeta\) I do not derive; \(\theta_\rho=\pi-2\arctan2\gamma\) is about \(1/\gamma\), on-line points of height below about \(n\) contribute order one each and those above about \(n^2/(2\gamma^2)\) each, and the reviewer recalls the size \((n/2)\log n\); none of this is used.

No cancellation in the limit, at an unreachable index. Part 3 shows that pairs cannot cancel each other for all \(n\): the largest modulus wins along a sequence of indices on which the leading pairs' phases align. Such indices recur with bounded gaps, but the first alignment can be far beyond \(1/\tau\) when several pairs share the largest modulus. Along them \(\lambda_n\le-(\tfrac12-o(1))M_*r_*^n\): a finite configuration with an off-line point violates every lower bound \(\lambda_n\ge-ce^{\epsilon n}\) with \(\epsilon<\log r_*\). The criterion thus has the continuation criterion's virtue only asymptotically, and at indices of order \(T^2/\delta\) for a zero at height \(T\).

Comparison with the other forms. In the Gram form an off-line pair's weight grows like \(e^{\delta\Lambda}\) in the bandwidth and is visible, against local counts, at \(\Lambda\) of order \(\log T\); here its weight grows like \(e^{n\delta/\gamma^2}\) in the index. The two are the same exponential in different parametrizations, \(n/\gamma^2\) playing the role of \(\Lambda\): an index \(n\) probes, at height \(\gamma\), the bandwidth \(n/\gamma^2\), and the bandwidth \(\log T\) corresponds to \(n\) of order \(T^2\log T\), the logarithm of part 2. I state this as an analogy read off the two formulas, not as a transfer of any estimate.

Sub-ideas

  • Read a source for the arithmetic expression of the coefficients for \(\zeta\) (the formula of Bombieri and Lagarias through the Weil explicit formula, or an arXiv treatment of Li coefficients) in its statements, and record which sum over primes and which archimedean term \(\lambda_n\) equals, what is known unconditionally about \(\lambda_n\) for large \(n\), and whether any one-sided bound at \(n\) of order \(T^2\) is weaker than a zero-free statement at height \(T\). Also decide whether \(\lambda_n\) is the Weil functional at a specific test function, in which case Li's positivity is an instance of the positivity of the first lead and the analogy with the bandwidth is an identity. Not done in this entry: the paper is not on arXiv to my knowledge and locating a readable source is a cycle's reading, which the lead's next entry is for.

Follow-up. Continuing: the lead asked whether Li's positivity gives a criterion of another kind. For finite configurations it is equivalent to all points being on the line, with the threshold index of part 2. Whether anything is known about the coefficients at the indices that matter is not examined, and the lead's sources are unread; that is the sub-idea and the next attempt on this lead. Record search. For it the registry was searched for Li criterion coefficients positivity off-line zero threshold growth, and for Keiper Bombieri Lagarias lambda_n explicit formula test function Weil positivity: no record on these objects. Within this notebook the nearest records are the Gram form's positivity statements (lem:pair-atom-spectrum, prop:extreme-eigenvalue-detection); whether Li's coefficients are an instance of them is the sub-idea's second question.

Reviewer's suggestions, unverified

  • Bombieri and Lagarias, Theorem 1 (from memory): for a multiset with a convergence condition, all real parts at most \(1/2\) is equivalent to the real-part sums being nonnegative, and to their being at least \(-c(\epsilon)e^{\epsilon n}\) for every \(\epsilon>0\); so a one-sided subexponential bound is already the goal. Record. No registry record; its finite analogue is the last sentence added to the paragraph on cancellation, from part 3.
  • Their Theorem 2 (from memory): an unconditional arithmetic formula for \(\lambda_n\) as a binomial combination of constants of \(-\zeta'/\zeta\) at \(1\) plus an explicit archimedean part, and \(\lambda_n\) as the Weil functional at a specific test function. Record. No registry record; added to the sub-idea.
  • Lagarias and Voros (from memory): the archimedean part is \((n/2)\log n+cn+O(1)\) unconditionally, the prime part \(O(\sqrt n\log n)\) under the goal, with exponentially growing oscillation otherwise; so \(S_{\mathrm{on}}(n)\) is about \((n/2)\log n\) and the threshold is \(n\) about \((\gamma^2/\delta)\log n\). Record. Searched Keiper Bombieri Lagarias lambda_n explicit formula test function Weil positivity, and Li criterion coefficients positivity off-line zero threshold growth: no match on these objects.
  • A result attributed to Oesterlé (from memory): the goal verified to height \(T\) gives \(\lambda_n\ge0\) for \(n\le T^2\), consistent with the threshold \(\gamma^2/\delta\). Record. Searched Keiper Bombieri Lagarias lambda_n explicit formula test function Weil positivity, and Li criterion coefficients positivity off-line zero threshold growth: no match on these objects.
  • No converse is known to the reviewer from positivity up to \(N\) to zero-freeness up to height \(\sqrt N\); the factor \(\cos n\varphi\) and cancellation at finite \(n\) block the naive one. Record. Searched Keiper Bombieri Lagarias lambda_n explicit formula test function Weil positivity, and Li criterion coefficients positivity off-line zero threshold growth: no match on these objects.

Suggestion check. The finite analogue of the first suggestion is verified from part 3, as stated in the text. The reviewer's counterexample to the unqualified reading is verified: for one quadruple and no on-line points, at an index with \(n\varphi\) a multiple of \(2\pi\) the pair's term is \(2-r^n-r^{-n}<0\), and \(\varphi\) is about \(1/\gamma\). The recalled theorems are not verified and nothing rests on them.

Verification gate. No dependency of the record is used in the proof. One fresh-context medium reviewer checked the three parts and the degenerate cases and found the proposition correct, and required corrections to its reading, all incorporated: the sentence that positivity up to \(N\) constrains only pairs with \(\gamma^2/\delta\lesssim N\) was false without the proviso on the on-line contribution, by the entry's own degenerate case; the per-pair threshold was misstated where part 2 gives it with \(\tau_{\max}\); the remark on recurrence of aligned indices was misleading; the case of rational angles in the approximation step and the cases \(M_{\mathrm{off}}=0\), \(S_{\mathrm{on}}=0\) were not covered in the wording; the description of the excluded points was wrong for \(0\). 11 October 2026 — Second route review: the three leads are the zero-free statement in the forms examined so far and none is closed; the next test asks what a positivity certificate at a point can give

Route review; no result about zeta zeros. Second exclusion review. Produced by Claude Code (claude-opus-5-5, low effort).

New mathematics. None registered. Three cheap numerical tests were run (heat flow, power sums, truncation), two of which test recalled theorems and not the translations; and one bound is derived in the Architecture review for the next cycle's test: a continuous even real \(g\) whose transform has nonnegative real part on the whole critical strip has \(|g(u)|\le g(0)/\cosh(u/2)\).

Goal progress. Unchanged. The six research entries since the last review each reported the obligation unchanged, and the turn guidance at this cycle's start counts ten research entries in a row declaring no bearing at the goal's parameters. Bearing. This review gives nothing at the goal's parameters; it selects the next test.

General statement. The line's claim is the open statement itself, off-line exclusion: for every nontrivial zero, \(\Re\rho=1/2\). No registered conjecture stands between the proved statements and it; the stage-one reviewer notes that the route therefore has no stated target statement of its own.

What the six entries did. The family optimum entry answered the last review's test: the seven-eighths source's second stage is not a bootstrap, and its scales are within \(5\cdot10^{-5}\) of its family's optimum under listed extrapolations. The other five developed the last review's three leads. Weil positivity: the Gram form's extreme eigenvalues see a solitary pair at bandwidth \(\log T\) in the model (extreme eigenvalue detection), and the prime side of the form gives only the trivial bound by the elementary route (prime-side form). Hermite–Biehler: the half-plane sign, the count of real zeros and the modulus inequality at each shift are the zero-free statement, and the local phase condition cannot fail beyond half the local gap (sign criteria, structure functions). Li: in the model a pair matters from index about \(\gamma^2/\delta\) (Li threshold). Two of these entries drafted closures of their leads that their reviewers did not uphold; all three leads remain queued with sub-ideas.

What the goal needs. An inequality that a hypothetical zero at \(\beta_0+i\gamma_0\), \(\beta_0>1/2\), violates, proved without knowledge of the other zeros, at every height. Density and proportion statements do not give it.

Falsification attempt. Against the line's working belief that some reformulation will expose a weaker premise: every criterion examined in this record is, in the form in which it is an equivalence, the zero-free statement, and in the form in which it is weaker (a second moment, a local phase condition, finitely many coefficients) it does not see the zero. The five entries on the three leads tried this pattern on new criteria and none broke it. This is my summary of the entries, not the result of a search. Against the imports: the stage-one reviewer recalls that no zero-free half-plane is known in the published literature, so the seven-eighths import contradicts the state of the art as remembered and everything conditional on it must say so; this notebook has read its criterion and its scales and has not audited its estimates. A third check, from memory and unread: functions with a functional equation and no Euler product are recalled to have zeros off the line, so any mechanism must use the primes; the finite-configuration equivalences of this notebook do not, which is consistent with their being restatements.

Assessment of this line. Weak. The line has no mechanism: nothing recorded supplies an estimate that does not presuppose the zeros' location, and the six entries produced correct small statements about models, each of which a reviewer had to trim. The evidence that changed since the last review is negative: the continuation mechanism has no slack in its family, and the three leads are restatements in the forms examined. What remains untested is narrow and listed under Follow-ups. I do not give odds; the honest reading of the record is that the notebook has mapped reformulations and has not found an approach.

Obstacle

The line is stuck on one thing: an unconditional inequality with a power saving. In every formulation the off-line zero's contribution has size \(e^{\delta\Lambda}\) at a scale \(\Lambda\) (bandwidth, shift, index divided by \(\gamma^2\)), and the term it must beat, which comes from the pole of \(\zeta\) or from the prime sum of length \(e^{\Lambda}\) bounded trivially, has size \(e^{\Lambda/2}\). Beating it for some \(\delta<1/2\) is a power saving in a prime sum of length about \(T^{\theta}\) at height \(T\), or a test function for which the pole's term is small while the positivity that controls the other zeros is kept; in that second formulation the stage-two review points out that the pole's term can be made zero and the binding terms are the archimedean term and the actual zeros near the point. The second half, cancellation among several off-line zeros, is secondary: it is settled for separated pairs and costs a range of bandwidths for clusters.

Outside leads

  • Positivity certificates of Odlyzko type. Named source, recalled and unread: the explicit-formula method for discriminant bounds (Odlyzko, Serre, Poitou), which uses test functions whose transform has nonnegative real part on the whole strip, so that all zeros, wherever they are, contribute nonnegatively; and its linear-programming form, as in the Cohn–Elkies bound for sphere packing. Target: off-line exclusion at a point. Translation: find continuous even real \(g\) supported in \([-\Lambda,\Lambda]\) with \(g(\log n)\ge0\) at prime powers and \(\Re h\ge0\) on \(|\Im z|\le1/2\) for \(h=\widehat g\), such that the hypothetical quadruple's term \(4\Re h(\gamma_0+i\delta)\) exceeds the pole's term \(2\int g(u)\cosh(u/2)\,du\) plus the archimedean term; the explicit formula would then exclude a zero at \(1/2+\delta+i\gamma_0\) whatever the other zeros are. It is the classical explicit-formula route to a zero-free region in its general form, and it would break if positivity on the strip prevents \(h\) from separating the point from the true zeros near it. Answers. It is an inequality a single off-line zero violates with no hypothesis on the others, with the pole's term left to the choice of \(g\). Test. Passed as a formulation only: the constraints are linear in \(g\) and the bound \(|g(u)|\cosh(u/2)\le g(0)\) derived below does not by itself make the program infeasible. The stage-two reviewer proposes an obstruction that would limit it to the classical region; proving or breaking that is the Decisive test.
  • Turán's power-sum method. Named theorem, Turán's second main theorem as recalled and unread: for \(z_1,\dots,z_N\) with \(\max|z_j|=1\), \(\max_{m<\nu\le m+N}|\sum_jz_j^{\nu}|\ge(N/(8e(m+N)))^{N}\). Target: the cluster half of the obstacle. Translation: the zero sums of the Gram model and Li's coefficients are power sums in the bandwidth or the index. Answers. It would bound from below, over \(N\) consecutive scales, what a cluster of off-line zeros contributes. Test. Falsified at its own break point. The recalled inequality was not violated in 2400 configurations (\(N\le8\), \(m\le50\), half built to cancel; least ratio to the bound \(419\); output), which tests my memory of the theorem and not the translation. The translation breaks by arithmetic: the sum runs over all zeros in the window, and already with \(N=\log T\) the factor \((N/(8e(m+N)))^{N}\) is at most \((8e)^{-\log T}=T^{-3.08}\), against a pair's weight of at most \(T^{1/2}\) at bandwidth \(\log T\).
  • The de Bruijn–Newman heat flow. Named theorems, recalled and unread: de Bruijn's, that zeros in a strip of half-width \(\Delta\) become real after time \(\Delta^2/2\), and the bound \(\Lambda\ge0\) of Rodgers and Tao. As a criterion it is the goal verbatim, and the stage-one reviewer rejects it for that reason. Its proposed use here was as a filter on methods: a criterion that still holds after a small backward flow cannot prove the goal. Answers. It does not answer the obstacle; it would test proposed answers. Test. Not run: the filter use needs a recorded candidate premise that makes sense for the flowed function, which has no Euler product, and none is named. What was run checks de Bruijn's theorem in a polynomial model: for a pair at \(\delta\in\{0.2,0.5,1\}\) among seven real points at spacing \(1\) or \(3\), all roots of \(e^{-tD^2}p\) are real from a time between \(0.32\) and \(0.97\) of \(\delta^2/2\), earlier when the real points are denser; a backward flow of time \(0.2\) on the all-real configuration gives non-real roots, and times \(0.02\), \(0.05\), \(0.1\) do not.
  • The Nyman–Beurling criterion. Named theorem, recalled and unread: the goal holds if and only if the indicator of \((0,1)\) is an \(L^2\) limit of combinations \(\sum c_k\{\theta_k/x\}\) with \(\sum c_k\theta_k=0\). For a zero \(\rho\) with \(\beta>1/2\) the Mellin transform at \(\rho\) is a bounded functional vanishing on the combinations (by the recalled formula for the transform of \(\{\theta/x\}\)), with value \(1/\rho\) at the indicator and norm \(1/\sqrt{2\beta-1}\), which gives distance at least \(\sqrt{2\beta-1}/|\rho|\). Answers. It would replace an estimate by a construction. Test. Falsified as an answer at height \(T\): excluding a zero needs distance below \(\sqrt{2\delta}/T\), while the recalled rate of the best approximations with dilations up to \(N\) is of order \(1/\sqrt{\log N}\), so \(N\) of order \(e^{cT^2/\delta}\); the same scale as Li's index.
  • Littlewood's lemma. Named theorem, recalled and unread: \(2\pi\sum_{\beta>\sigma}(\beta-\sigma)\) over zeros up to \(T\) equals the integral of \(\log|\zeta(\sigma+it)|\) plus \(O(\log T)\). Answers. It would turn exclusion into a mean value of \(\log|\zeta|\). Test. Falsified: one zero contributes at most \(\pi\) to the left side and the error term is of order \(\log T\); the identity is a density statement.

Absurd bridges

  • Robust stability in control theory. Translation: the shifted function \(\xi(1/2+h-iz)\) is stable (zeros in a half-plane) exactly when no zero lies right of the shifted line; Kharitonov's theorem gives stability of a family of polynomials from finitely many members, and stability criteria act on coefficients. A theorem there would give zero-freeness from finitely many coefficient conditions on truncations. Smallest test: does stability survive truncation of the Taylor expansion? Answers. It would replace an estimate at height \(T\) by conditions on coefficients. Test. Falsified for the encoding by Taylor sections at \(0\), which is not Kharitonov's setting of coefficient intervals: for random on-line configurations of degree 6, 10, 14 shifted by \(h\in\{0.1,0.6,2\}\), the sections have zeros in the closed upper half-plane in eight of nine cases, in each of them at every section of degree at least 5 (output).
  • The Lee–Yang theorem of statistical mechanics. Translation: \(\Xi\) as the Fourier transform of a measure with the Lee–Yang property, whose cumulants then alternate in sign (Newman, recalled and unread). The cumulants of a configuration are the power sums \(\sum_\rho z_\rho^{-2k}\). A theorem there would give reality of zeros from a ferromagnetic structure of the measure. Smallest test: at which \(k\) does an off-line pair change the sign of a power sum? Answers. It would give a structural reason for reality that uses no estimate. Test. Falsified as a detector at height \(T\): a pair at \(\gamma\pm i\delta\) contributes \(2|z|^{-2k}\cos(2k\arctan(\delta/\gamma))\), negative first near \(k=\pi\gamma/(4\delta)\), where its weight relative to the lowest zero is \((\gamma_1/\gamma)^{2k}\); the sign conditions see only the lowest zeros.
  • Weight enumerators in coding theory. Translation: the functional equation as a MacWilliams identity and the zeta polynomial of a self-dual code (Duursma, recalled and unread), whose zeros lie on a circle for extremal codes; extremality would be the ingredient to translate. Answers. It would name a combinatorial property, beyond the functional equation and positivity, that forces the zeros onto the line. Test. Not run: the test is to read the source's statements on which codes satisfy the analogue and why, which is a cycle's reading.

Follow-ups

The reviewers' unqueued suggestions kept for this review: the disc statement with a measure version from density estimates is dropped, being a density statement by its own description; Jensen polynomials and Turán inequalities are dropped without a test, by analogy with two other coefficient families (the Li threshold and the Lee–Yang power sums above), which is a one-step objection and is recorded as such; the de Bruijn–Newman constant is the third lead above, not run.

Architecture review

Necessary target. For each \(\beta_0\in(1/2,1)\) and each height \(\gamma_0\): an inequality, proved unconditionally, that a zero at \(\beta_0+i\gamma_0\) violates. It may depend on the point; no statement about simplicity or about the zeros on the line is needed. Downstream. Through the proved statements: by prop:shifted-structure-criterion and prop:log-derivative-sign-criteria the half-plane forms at shift \(h\) are equivalent to the absence of zeros right of \(1/2+h\), so a proof of any of them for all \(h>0\) is the goal and for one \(h\) is a zero-free half-plane, weaker; by prop:extreme-eigenvalue-detection an eigenvalue bound \(T^{\varepsilon}\) at bandwidth \(\log T\) gives \(\delta\le\varepsilon+o(1)\) for a solitary pair in the model, weaker than the goal and conditional on a transfer not made.

Extra requirements. The approaches so far asked for more than the target in three ways. They asked for uniform statements (an operator norm, a sign on a half-plane) where the target is pointwise. They asked for positivity of the other zeros' contributions under the hypothesis that those lie on the line, which is why clusters and solitary pairs had to be separated; a test function with nonnegative real part on the whole strip needs no such hypothesis. And they bounded the pole's or primes' term by its trivial size instead of choosing the test function. The certificate formulation weakens all three; what it pays for the second is the subject of the Decisive test.

Independent alternative. Stage one, with the route withheld (stage1.txt): the reviewer found no architecture that escapes the standing observation and offered two candidates. A bootstrap on the supremum of zero abscissae through the continuation criterion: its open requirement, a new probe family, is the one recorded in family optimum, where the source's second stage was found to use the earlier bound only as a threshold; I know of no such family and made no search for one. A second moment on the prime side at bandwidth beyond \(\log T\): its open requirement, a saving \(T^{-\varepsilon}\), is the one recorded in bandwidth reading; the reviewer's consistency test at \(\lambda=4/3\) is not run and is a sub-idea below. Neither proposal is refuted; both restate requirements already recorded as open. The registered open conjectures of the route: none. The reviewer also audited the brief: its standing observation is my summary of the entries and not the result of a search, and the seven-eighths import should be marked as contradicting the literature as remembered. Stage two, with the route revealed (stage2.txt): pass with corrections; the comparison stands, and the reviewer proposes an answer to the Decisive test, below.

Rank normalization. The question applies in one place: the Gram form's negative direction was measured against \(\lambda_{\max}\) of the on-line part, an absolute size, and the pair's weight against the trace or the Hilbert–Schmidt norm of the whole form. In the certificate formulation both sides are values of one linear functional of \(g\), so the comparison is relative by construction; no dimension count remains.

Decision. Test the positivity certificate at a point, by deciding analytically what it can give. It is the one formulation found in which the inequality is pointwise and needs no hypothesis on the other zeros. Retained beside it: the continuation mechanism as the only recorded source of a power saving, which this notebook cannot improve inside its family.

Decisive test. Prove or break the following, the stage-two reviewer's suggestion, unverified. Setting: continuous even real \(g\) with compact support, \(h=\widehat g\), \(\Re h\ge0\) on the closed strip \(|\Im z|\le1/2\); since \(\Re h(t+iy)=\int g(u)\cosh(yu)\cos(tu)\,du\) is bounded and harmonic in the strip, the condition on the boundary implies it inside. What is derived here: by Bochner's theorem \(g(u)\cosh(u/2)\) is positive definite, so \(|g(u)|\cosh(u/2)\le g(0)\) and \(\Re h(\gamma_0+i\delta)\le g(0)\int_{\mathbb R}\cosh(\delta u)/\cosh(u/2)\,du=2\pi g(0)/\cos(\pi\delta)\); and the pole's term is \(2\widehat G(0)\) for \(G=g\cosh(\cdot/2)\), the boundary density at one point, which can be made zero, so the pole is not the obstruction. The statement to test: \(\Re h\) is the Poisson integral of its nonnegative boundary values, so by Harnack's inequality \(\Re h(\gamma)\ge c\cos(\pi\delta)e^{-\pi d}\Re h(\gamma_0+i\delta)\) for real \(\gamma\) within \(d\) of \(\gamma_0\); the explicit formula being an identity, a certificate needs \(4\Re h(\gamma_0+i\delta)\) to exceed the sum of \(\Re h\) over the actual zeros; the actual zeros within distance \(d\) of height \(\gamma_0\) number about \((d/\pi)\log\gamma_0\); hence a certificate can exist only if \(\cos(\pi\delta)\log\gamma_0\le C\), that is \(1/2-\delta\le C'/\log\gamma_0\), the classical region. Outcomes: if the lemma holds as stated, including for neighbours that are themselves off the line, the lead closes with a structural reason that applies to every method requiring nonnegativity on the whole strip, and the route must give up that positivity near \(\gamma_0\); if it breaks (the reviewer notes the argument weakens when neighbours may lie near the strip's edge), the entry states what a certificate can give unconditionally. Power. Recorded limits on the test's object: the trivial size \(e^{\Lambda/2}\) of the pole's and primes' terms (lem:prime-side-form-bound) applies to nonnegative test functions and not to this class; the bound \(2\pi g(0)/\cos(\pi\delta)\) derived here is finite for \(\delta<1/2\) and does not decide the question; the proposed lemma would. The test is a proof attempt with no computation: a numerical program at low height, which the first draft of this review proposed, would only certify what is known there and is dropped. The two outcomes differ in what the route may still require of a test function. Record search. The registry was searched for explicit formula positivity certificate linear programming test function strip Odlyzko, and for zero-free region explicit formula trigonometric polynomial: no record on these objects; the classical proofs of the zero-free region by this method (de la Vallée Poussin and later refinements) are recalled and unread. The earlier review's test (the second stage as a map of the assumed bound) is answered by the family optimum entry and is a different object.

Proposed next step. Route item: rh-exclusion. The Decisive test above, as a research entry on the picked lead of this review, with an Architecture test paragraph.

11 October 2026 — A test function that is nonnegative on the whole strip cannot separate a point from the zeros near it: a certificate excluding \(1/2+\delta+i\gamma_0\) needs the weighted count of actual zeros near \(\gamma_0\) to be below \(4/\cos(\pi\delta)\)

Working proof of an elementary obstruction, with a numerical control; it limits a method and proves nothing about zeta zeros. Strip positivity obstruction. Produced by Claude Code (claude-opus-5-5, low effort).

Chosen. This entry takes the second review's picked lead, positivity certificates, ahead of the queue's head, as that review's Decisive test requires. It bears on off-line exclusion: the certificate was the one formulation found in which a single off-line zero violates an inequality with no hypothesis on the others. At the goal's parameters the test could only tell whether that formulation can work at fixed distance from the line; a no gives nothing toward the goal and removes a class of methods.

Architecture test. The second review asked to prove or break its reviewer's proposed obstruction. Outcome: it holds, with explicit constants and without the explicit formula. For a test function with nonnegative real part on the closed strip, \(\Re h(\gamma+iy)\ge\cos(\pi y)\cos(\pi\delta)e^{-\pi|\gamma-\gamma_0|}\Re h(\gamma_0+i\delta)\); so any sound upper bound for the sum of \(\Re h\) over the actual zeros is at least \(\cos(\pi\delta)D(\gamma_0)\Re h(\gamma_0+i\delta)\), with \(D\) a weighted count of actual zeros near \(\gamma_0\), and a certificate needs \(\cos(\pi\delta)D(\gamma_0)<4\). The case the reviewer flagged, neighbours that are themselves off the line, is covered by the factor \(\cos(\pi y)\) in \(D\): the obstruction weakens as the nearby zeros approach the edge of the strip.

Question. The missing implication is an estimate excluding one off-line zero. Can a test function \(g\) with \(\Re\widehat g\ge0\) on \(|\Im z|\le1/2\) certify, through an upper bound for the sum over zeros, that \(1/2+\delta+i\gamma_0\) is not a zero, at fixed \(\delta<1/2\) and large height?

New mathematics. One lemma and its corollary. The lemma is Harnack's inequality in the strip with the constant written out; the corollary says that the method of positivity on the whole strip can exclude a point only where \(\cos(\pi\delta)\sum_\rho\cos(\pi y_\rho)e^{-\pi|\gamma_\rho-\gamma_0|}<4\), the sum over the actual zeros \(1/2+y_\rho+i\gamma_\rho\). The proposal came from the review's reviewer. Whether the application is in the literature I have not searched; it is Harnack's inequality in the strip, presumably folklore, and no novelty is claimed.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The one formulation the review found that needs no hypothesis on the other zeros is limited, by its own positivity, to points whose distance from the edge of the strip is at most of the order of the reciprocal of the weighted local count of zeros. If the zeros near \(\gamma_0\) are on the line and as numerous as the classical count (recalled, not read: about \(\tfrac1{2\pi}\log\gamma_0\) per unit height), this is \(1/2-\delta\lesssim4\pi/\log\gamma_0\), the shape of the classical region. So, given a lower bound of order \(\log\gamma_0\) for the number of zeros in every window of bounded length (the classical local count, recalled and not read in this cycle), the method cannot prove the goal: where the goal holds nearby, it cannot reach fixed \(\delta\). One exception is noted below: zeros near \(\gamma_0\) that have been located can be subtracted. Bearing. At the goal's parameters this gives nothing; it is a limit of a method. The cycle was spent on it because the review's decision depended on it.

General statement. lem:strip-positivity-obstruction, for every continuous even real \(g\) of compact support whose transform has nonnegative real part on the lines \(\Im z=\pm1/2\), every pair of points of the open strip, and every multiset of points of the strip.

Review. The stand-in review after the route review raised five objections. (1) Three entries in a row admitted a mistake with a Prevention that changed nothing, and I declined to build the closure check instead of building and measuring it: accepted and fixed. The finisher now requires of every closure a paragraph on what the item names that was not examined; measured on all notebooks by a scratch count whose output the framework commit's message quotes, 217 of 2145 research entries close an item and 4 of those would lack only this paragraph. (2) A test of a recalled inequality in 2400 configurations distinguishes nothing and should not be recorded as passed: accepted; the committed review records that lead as falsified at its break point and does not pick it; the chat table that said passed predates the review's second stage. (3) The Stop-hook pattern was committed with three false hits among twelve read and thirty unread: fixed; the pattern now needs a noun of the kind withdrawn, and all 33 remaining matches on this machine's transcripts, read, take back a statement. (4) A source not retrieved goes to the user's request file, and none was filed: fixed; a request for three papers is filed with citations, what was tried and the defaults followed. (5) "I know of no estimate" is a belief: accepted; the Li entry as committed labels it an untested hypothesis, and the route review's assessment is stated as a summary of the entries, not a search.

Statement and proof

Let \(g:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R\) be continuous, even, of compact support, and \(h(z)=\int g(u)e^{izu}\,du\). For real \(t\) and \(|y|\le1/2\), since \(g\) is even, \(\Re h(t+iy)=\int g(u)\cosh(yu)\cos(tu)\,du\). Put \(G(u)=g(u)\cosh(u/2)\) and \(\varphi(s)=\Re h(s\pm i/2)=\widehat G(s)\). Let \[K_y(t)={2\pi\cos(\pi y)\cosh(\pi t)\over\sinh^2(\pi t)+\cos^2(\pi y)}\qquad(|y|<1/2).\]

Lemma (strip positivity obstruction). lem:strip-positivity-obstruction. Suppose \(\varphi\ge0\) on \(\mathbb R\). Then:

  1. \(\varphi\) is integrable, and for \(|y|<1/2\), \(\Re h(t+iy)=\tfrac1{2\pi}\int\varphi(s)K_y(t-s)\,ds\); in particular \(\Re h\ge0\) on the whole closed strip.
  2. For all real \(\gamma,\gamma_0\) and \(|y|,|\delta|<1/2\): \(\Re h(\gamma+iy)\ge\cos(\pi y)\cos(\pi\delta)\,e^{-\pi|\gamma-\gamma_0|}\,\Re h(\gamma_0+i\delta)\).
  3. Let \(Z\) be a multiset of points \(\gamma_\rho+iy_\rho\) of the open strip and \(B\) a real number with \(\sum_{\rho\in Z}\Re h(\gamma_\rho+iy_\rho)\le B\). Put \(D_Z(\gamma_0)=\sum_{\rho\in Z}\cos(\pi y_\rho)e^{-\pi|\gamma_\rho-\gamma_0|}\). If \(c\,\Re h(\gamma_0+i\delta)>B\) for some \(c>0\), then \(\cos(\pi\delta)D_Z(\gamma_0)<c\).

Proof. (1) \(G\) is continuous with compact support and \(\widehat G=\varphi\ge0\). For \(\epsilon>0\), \(\int\varphi(s)e^{-\epsilon s^2}ds=\int G(u)\sqrt{\pi/\epsilon}\,e^{-u^2/4\epsilon}du\), which tends to \(2\pi G(0)\) as \(\epsilon\to0\) by continuity of \(G\); by monotone convergence \(\int\varphi=2\pi G(0)<\infty\). Hence Fourier inversion holds at every point: \(G(u)=\tfrac1{2\pi}\int\varphi(s)\cos(su)\,ds\). The function \(m_y(u)=\cosh(yu)/\cosh(u/2)\) is integrable for \(|y|<1/2\), and \(\int m_y(u)\cos(tu)\,du=K_y(t)\). Proof of this: for real \(w\) let \(I(w)=\int e^{iwu}/\cosh(u/2)\,du\). Since \(\cosh((u+2\pi i)/2)=-\cosh(u/2)\), integrating over the rectangle with sides \(\Im u=0\) and \(\Im u=2\pi\) gives \(I(w)(1+e^{-2\pi w})=2\pi i\operatorname{Res}_{u=i\pi}\), and the residue is \(e^{-\pi w}/(\tfrac12\sinh(i\pi/2))=-2ie^{-\pi w}\), so \(I(w)=4\pi e^{-\pi w}/(1+e^{-2\pi w})=2\pi/\cosh(\pi w)\). Both sides are analytic in \(|\Im w|<1/2\), where the integral converges absolutely, so the identity holds there. For \(w=t-iy\) the integrand is \(e^{yu}e^{itu}/\cosh(u/2)\), whose integral has real part \(\int m_y(u)\cos(tu)\,du\) (the odd parts integrate to zero); and \(\cosh(\pi t-i\pi y)=\cosh\pi t\cos\pi y-i\sinh\pi t\sin\pi y\) has squared modulus \(\sinh^2\pi t+\cos^2\pi y\), so \(\Re\,2\pi/\cosh(\pi(t-iy))=K_y(t)\). The control below checks the formula numerically as well. Then \(\Re h(t+iy)=\int G(u)m_y(u)\cos(tu)\,du=\tfrac1{2\pi}\int\varphi(s)\int m_y(u)\cos(su)\cos(tu)\,du\,ds\) by Fubini, and \(\int m_y\cos(su)\cos(tu)\,du=\tfrac12(K_y(t-s)+K_y(t+s))\); as \(\varphi\) is even the two halves agree. \(K_y>0\) gives the positivity. (2) With \(c=\cos(\pi y)\in(0,1]\): \(K_y(t)/K_0(t)=c\cosh^2(\pi t)/(\sinh^2(\pi t)+c^2)\), which is at least \(c\) because \(\sinh^2+c^2\le\cosh^2\), and at most \(1/c\) because \(c^2\cosh^2\le\sinh^2+c^2\). And \(K_0(t)=2\pi/\cosh(\pi t)\) satisfies \(K_0(t+d)\ge e^{-\pi|d|}K_0(t)\), since \(\cosh(a+b)\le e^{|b|}\cosh a\). Hence \(K_y(t+d)\ge\cos(\pi y)K_0(t+d)\ge\cos(\pi y)e^{-\pi|d|}K_0(t)\ge\cos(\pi y)\cos(\pi\delta)e^{-\pi|d|}K_\delta(t)\) for all real \(t,d\). Integrating against \(\varphi\ge0\) with \(d=\gamma-\gamma_0\) gives (2). (3) By (1) every term of the sum is nonnegative, so \(B\ge0\), hence \(\Re h(\gamma_0+i\delta)>0\); by (2), \(B\ge\sum_\rho\Re h(\gamma_\rho+iy_\rho)\ge\cos(\pi\delta)D_Z(\gamma_0)\Re h(\gamma_0+i\delta)\), and dividing by \(\Re h(\gamma_0+i\delta)\) gives \(c>\cos(\pi\delta)D_Z(\gamma_0)\). \(\square\)

Degenerate cases. \(g\equiv0\): both sides of (2) vanish and (3) has no instance. \(Z\) empty: \(D_Z=0\) and (3) is no restriction, as it should be when there are no zeros to protect. \(\delta\to1/2\): the factor \(\cos(\pi\delta)\) tends to \(0\) and the restriction disappears, consistent with the edge of the strip being reachable. \(\delta=0\): a point on the line can be certified only where \(D_Z(\gamma_0)<c\). A function \(g\) that is not even, or not real, is outside the lemma as stated, and it is not an evasion: the zero set is invariant under \(z\mapsto-z\) and \(z\mapsto\bar z\), so the sum over zeros sees only the symmetrized \(g\), whose \(\Re h\) is an average of \(\Re h\) at four points of the strip, still nonnegative, with the same value at the image quadruple. Points of \(Z\) on the boundary lines are outside the statement and would contribute nonnegative terms that only strengthen (3). Compact support is used only to make \(G\) integrable; the proof works for continuous even real \(g\) with \(g\cosh(u/2)\) integrable. The constant in (2) is sharp in each factor separately at \(y=0\) (\(\cos\pi\delta\) at \(t=d=0\); \(e^{-\pi|d|}\) as \(t\to\pm\infty\) with \(\delta=0\)) and not jointly: for \(y=\delta\), \(d=0\) the true ratio is \(1\) against the bound \(\cos^2\pi\delta\).

What it says about certificates

The method. A certificate that \(1/2+\delta+i\gamma_0\) is not a zero, of the kind the review described, consists of an admissible \(g\) and a number \(B\) such that the explicit formula, with the sign of the prime term used, proves \(\sum_\rho\Re h(z_\rho)\le B\) for the actual zeros \(z_\rho\) in the variable \(z\); a zero at the point with its three images would add \(4\Re h(\gamma_0+i\delta)\), and the certificate succeeds if that exceeds \(B\). (For \(\delta\ne0\) and \(\gamma_0\ne0\) the four images contribute equal real parts, \(\Re h\) being even in \(t\) and in \(y\); on the line or at \(\gamma_0=0\) there are two images, and a multiple zero would count more, so \(4\) is the case for a simple off-line zero.) Part 3 with \(c=4\) and \(Z\) the actual zeros: success requires \(\cos(\pi\delta)D(\gamma_0)<4\), where \(D(\gamma_0)=\sum_\rho\cos(\pi y_\rho)e^{-\pi|\gamma_\rho-\gamma_0|}\). The explicit formula's normalization does not enter: only that the certificate's bound is sound. The constraint on \(g\) at prime powers is not used either; it can only restrict further.

Size of the count. If the \(n\) zeros within distance \(1\) of height \(\gamma_0\) are on the line, \(D(\gamma_0)\ge ne^{-\pi}\). With the classical local count (recalled, not read) and all nearby zeros on the line, \(D(\gamma_0)\) is about \(\pi^{-2}\log(\gamma_0/2\pi)\), so success requires \(\cos(\pi\delta)\lesssim4\pi^2/\log\gamma_0\). Unconditionally the factors \(\cos(\pi y_\rho)\) can be small only for zeros near the edge of the strip; I do not derive a lower bound for \(D\) here. The structural reason is the one the reviewer named: positivity for all possible zeros is paid for by Harnack's inequality, and a positive harmonic function cannot be large at a point and small at the zeros within bounded distance of it.

What the obstruction does not cover. Test functions whose real part is allowed to be negative somewhere in the strip, with the zeros in that region controlled by other information; methods that bound the sum over zeros from both sides; and certificates for a different statement than the absence of a zero at a point. The first is the reviewer's suggested evasion and needs information about \(\zeta\) near height \(\gamma_0\). A fourth exception keeps positivity on the whole strip: if a set \(S\) of actual zeros near \(\gamma_0\) has been located, their terms can be computed and subtracted, part 3 applies to the remaining zeros, and the requirement becomes \(\cos(\pi\delta)D_{Z\setminus S}(\gamma_0)<4\); this is a device for verification at a finite height, of the kind used in numerical checks, and not a route to all heights. The linear-programming form the lead names is answered by the lemma under whole-strip positivity: it holds for every admissible \(g\), so for the optimum.

Numerical control

Nonvacuity. The script kernel_check.py checks three things that could each fail. The recalled integral: \(K_y(t)\) against quadrature of \(\int m_y(u)\cos(tu)\,du\) at sixteen points (\(y\le0.49\), \(t\le2.5\)), largest relative difference \(1.8\cdot10^{-10}\). The kernel inequality of part 2 on a grid of 147600 points: least ratio of the two sides \(1.0\), attained at the trivial point \(y=\delta=0\), \(d=0\), so this confirms the inequality and says nothing about sharpness. Part 2 end to end, computed from \(g\) without the kernel, for forty admissible functions \(g=G/\cosh(u/2)\) with \(G\) a triangle times \((1+\cos au)/2\), the hypothesis \(\varphi\ge0\) checked on a grid, at thirty random pairs of points each, all 1200 pairs used: least ratio \(1.22\) (output). The first run's quadrature was wrong (a truncated oscillatory integral, relative difference \(328\)); the Fourier-integral rule replaced it. The control is not registered as a claim.

Sub-ideas

  • Relax the positivity: require \(\Re h\ge0\) only outside a window of bounded width around \(\gamma_0\), and state what must then be known about the actual zeros inside the window for a certificate to succeed at fixed \(\delta\); decide whether that requirement is weaker than a zero-free region of the same width, or the same statement.

Follow-up. Continuing: the lead asked whether a positivity certificate at a point can exclude an off-line zero with no hypothesis on the others. With positivity on the whole strip it cannot at fixed distance from the line wherever the weighted count of actual zeros exceeds \(4/\cos(\pi\delta)\). The lead's statement also names the linear-programming form and the comparison with what a relaxed positivity needs; the relaxed form is the sub-idea and the next attempt on this lead. Record search. For it the registry was searched for explicit formula positivity certificate linear programming test function strip Odlyzko, for zero-free region explicit formula trigonometric polynomial, for Harnack Poisson kernel strip positive harmonic, and for relaxed positivity window local zero density certificate: no record on these objects.

Reviewer's suggestions, unverified

  • Relaxed positivity with only a count of the window's zeros gains nothing: if \(\Re h\ge-\eta\) in the window and the input is a count bound, then \(\Re h+\eta\) is nonnegative harmonic with the same Poisson representation and the requirement \(\cos(\pi\delta)D_W<4\) returns; any evasion needs the location of the window's zeros. Record. Searched relaxed positivity window local zero density certificate: no match; it is the next entry's first step.
  • Location information that would suffice is, for the line, the goal in the window except at the point; a formal intermediate is a zero-density bound for every bounded window, which density theorems give only for most heights; the known inputs for every height (local growth of \(\zeta\), cancellation in sums over primes) reach widths tending to zero. From the reviewer's memory. Record. The same search: no match.
  • A heuristic: success needs \(h\) small on average at about \(\log\gamma_0\) actual zeros per unit height while large at the point, which suggests exponential type at least \(\tfrac12\log\gamma_0\) with unsigned prime sums. Record. The same search: no match.
  • No named theorem is known to the reviewer for this application; nearest are the extremal problems for the trigonometric-polynomial method and dual limits of linear-programming bounds. Record. Searched zero-free region explicit formula trigonometric polynomial: no match.

Suggestion check. Verified and adopted in the text: the residue derivation of the kernel (the reviewer's two-line derivation used the transform of the hyperbolic secant, which is now proved in the entry); the symmetrization remark, since for real \(g\) with even part \(g_e\) the sum over a set invariant under \(z\mapsto-z\) of \(h\) equals that of the transform of \(g_e\); and the subtraction of located zeros, which is part 3 applied to the remaining multiset. The first suggestion in the list is not verified and is the next entry's test.

Verification gate. No dependency of the record is used in the proof. One fresh-context medium reviewer checked the three parts, the control and the reading, and found the lemma correct; corrections, all incorporated: the proof rested on a recalled integral checked only numerically (it is now derived by residues in the entry); the claim that the control showed the constant sharp came from a trivial grid point (replaced by the true statement: sharp in each factor separately, not jointly); "exactly" overstated a one-sided bound; the count \(c=4\) needed its cases; non-even or non-real \(g\) read as an evasion and is not one; an exception that keeps whole-strip positivity, subtracting located zeros, was missing; the conclusion that the method cannot prove the goal is conditional on a local count that is recalled, and is now stated so; compact support is not needed beyond integrability. The control's docstring did not match its code and skipped pairs were not counted (fixed and rerun: all 1200 pairs used, same numbers). Process audit. Findings, acted on: the status line said working proof over a recalled formula (now proved); "I believe the phenomenon is classical" was a belief (replaced by what was and was not searched); the lead's linear-programming form was named and left unresolved (now answered in the text); the sub-idea was not searched as a new statement (search added); the Prevention explained the instance and not the class (rewritten); the Review paragraph's counts had no source (now attributed to the framework commit's message, where the commands' outputs are quoted).

Process assessment. The lemma was the review's reviewer's proposal and took one short cycle once the explicit formula was seen not to be needed; the control's first quadrature was wrong and its own output showed it. Prevention. The class is a numerical check whose own method is unsound. This instance showed itself by an absurd number; a subtly wrong quadrature would not. The safeguard used here is an independent cross-check: the end-to-end test computes both sides from \(g\) without the kernel formula, so a wrong kernel would not pass both, and the formula is now also proved. I know no mechanical check for the soundness of a quadrature and add none; the framework changes of this turn, the closure paragraph and the tightened hook, answer the stand-in review and are described under Review.

11 October 2026 — Relaxing the positivity to \(-\eta\) in one window, paid for by one count, returns the same condition for the window's zeros; and under two classical count statements and an on-line fraction, the window's weighted count is of order \(\log T\) at a positive proportion of heights

Working proofs of two elementary statements, the second conditional on two count statements as given in a hypothesis file and on a fraction of on-line zeros; they limit a method in one model. Relaxed positivity obstruction. Produced by Claude Code (claude-opus-5-5, low effort).

Chosen. This entry continues the second review's picked lead ahead of the queue's head, as the previous entry's next step named (the queue tool's status: one entry so far, sub-idea open). It bears on off-line exclusion by deciding whether the certificate formulation escapes its obstruction when positivity is relaxed near the point. At the goal's parameters it could show that a count of nearby zeros, which is known, suffices where their location is not; if the obstruction returns, it gives nothing toward the goal.

Owed attempt. The previous entry on this lead named the relaxed form as the next attempt: positivity required only outside a window, and what a certificate then needs. It was made here; its outcome is part 2 of the lemma for one model, with the question's second half re-filed as a sub-idea.

Best outcome. Stated in the Chosen paragraph when the cycle opened: if a count of the zeros in a window sufficed where their location is not known, a relaxed certificate could succeed at fixed \(\delta\) using only the classical local count, which would be an unconditional exclusion of points at fixed distance from the line at the heights where it applies, a result of the kind the goal needs. The outcome found is the opposite, in the one model treated.

Question. The missing implication is an estimate excluding one off-line zero. (a) If \(\Re h\) may be as low as \(-\eta\) inside a window around \(\gamma_0\) and the certificate uses only an upper bound for the number of zeros there, can it succeed where the weighted count of actual zeros in the window is large? (b) What do statements read in this cycle give for that weighted count?

New mathematics. (a) In the model of one depth \(\eta\) and one count for the window: no, the requirement \(\cos(\pi\delta)D_W(\gamma_0)<4\) returns, with \(D_W\) the weighted count over the actual zeros in the window. (b) Under the Riemann–von Mangoldt count on \([T,2T]\) and the local upper count, as assumed in the density project's hypothesis file with their attribution to Titchmarsh: if at least a fraction \(\kappa>0\) of the zeros with ordinate in \((T+1,2T-1]\) is on the line, then \(D_W(\gamma_0)\ge c_W\kappa\log T\) on a subset of \([T,2T]\) of measure at least \(c'_W\kappa T\), with explicit constants. Both are elementary; no novelty is claimed.

Goal progress. Unchanged. With (a) the certificate formulation meets the same condition in a second form; other count-only models and hypotheses on the window's zeros weaker than their location are not examined. With (b) the condition is shown to fail, for a fixed window and conditionally on a positive on-line fraction, at a positive proportion of heights in \([T,2T]\) for points with \(\cos(\pi\delta)>8/m_W(T)\); a lower bound for the count in every window is still only recalled and is not used. Bearing. At the goal's parameters this gives nothing; it extends the limit of a method. The cycle was spent on it because the relaxed form was the previous entry's open sub-idea and the stand-in review asked that the count not rest on recollection.

General statement. lem:relaxed-positivity-obstruction, for every continuous even real \(g\) of compact support with integrable boundary function bounded below by \(-\eta\), every window, and every multiset of points of the strip; its second part for every \(T\) at least the source's threshold.

(1) A recalled zero count is not evidence: accepted; part (b) below derives an averaged count from two statements read in this cycle, and what remains recalled (a lower bound in every window) is labelled and unused. (2) The review called the line without mechanism while one lead was not run and another falsified by one objection: accepted; the two leads and the unrun bridge are queued for a development cycle each, by the queue tool, in the framework commit after the previous entry. (3) The chat report of the control's wrong quadrature had no Prevention paragraph: accepted; the entry had one and the chat report did not, which the Stop hook's patterns did not catch because the sentence used none of its words; the reply after this entry carries it. (4) The fix named for leads marked passed from memory did not address that class: accepted and fixed; the finisher now requires of every passed review item a statement of its break point and what the test found there; by a scratch count whose output that framework commit's message quotes, of 612 passed items in all notebooks' reviews none had one, so the check binds every new review. (5) "So this method cannot prove the hypothesis" was stated before review, on a recalled count, and for whole-strip positivity only: accepted; the committed entry states it conditionally, and this entry replaces the recalled count, for a positive proportion of heights, by two count statements assumed in a hypothesis file together with an on-line fraction as a hypothesis, and treats one model of the relaxed form.

Statement and proof

Notation as in the strip positivity obstruction: \(g\) continuous, even, real, of compact support, \(h=\widehat g\), \(u(t+iy)=\Re h(t+iy)\), \(\varphi(s)=u(s\pm i/2)\), and \(K_y\) the kernel there, for which \(\tfrac1{2\pi}\int K_y=1\) because \(K_y\) is the transform of \(m_y\) and \(m_y(0)=1\).

Lemma (relaxed positivity obstruction). lem:relaxed-positivity-obstruction.

  1. Suppose \(\varphi\) is integrable and \(\varphi\ge-\eta\) on \(\mathbb R\) for some \(\eta\ge0\). Then \(u\ge-\eta\) on the closed strip, and for all real \(\gamma,\gamma_0\) and \(|y|,|\delta|<1/2\): \(u(\gamma+iy)+\eta\ge\cos(\pi y)\cos(\pi\delta)e^{-\pi|\gamma-\gamma_0|}\,(u(\gamma_0+i\delta)+\eta)\).
  2. In addition let \(W>0\), suppose \(u\ge0\) at every point of the open strip with \(|\Re z-\gamma_0|>W\), let \(Z\) be a multiset of points of the open strip with \(\sum_{\rho\in Z}u(z_\rho)\le B\), let \(n_W\) be the number of its points with \(|\gamma_\rho-\gamma_0|\le W\) and \(D_W=\sum_{|\gamma_\rho-\gamma_0|\le W}\cos(\pi y_\rho)e^{-\pi|\gamma_\rho-\gamma_0|}\). If \(c\,u(\gamma_0+i\delta)>B+\eta N\) for some \(c>0\) and some \(N\ge n_W\), then \(\cos(\pi\delta)D_W<c\).
  3. Let \(Z\) be a multiset of points \(\gamma_\rho+iy_\rho\) of the strip, \(N(a,b)\) the number with \(a<\gamma_\rho\le b\), and suppose \(N(t,t+1)\le A_0\log(|t|+3)\) for all real \(t\) and \(|N(T,2T)-\tfrac T{2\pi}\ell(T)|\le C\log T\) for \(T\ge T_0\), with \(\ell(T)=\log\tfrac T{2\pi}+2\log2-1\). Let \(T\ge\max(T_0,2)\), \(W>0\), \(\omega=\min(W,1)\), and suppose at least \(\kappa N(T+1,2T-1)\) of the points with \(T+1<\gamma_\rho\le2T-1\) have \(y_\rho=0\). Then, with \(D_W(\gamma_0)=\sum_{|\gamma_\rho-\gamma_0|\le W}\cos(\pi y_\rho)e^{-\pi|\gamma_\rho-\gamma_0|}\), \[{1\over T}\int_T^{2T}D_W(\gamma_0)\,d\gamma_0\ \ge\ m_W(T):={2(1-e^{-\pi\omega})\kappa\over\pi}\Bigl({\ell(T)\over2\pi}-{C\log T+2A_0\log(2T+3)\over T}\Bigr),\] \(D_W(\gamma_0)\le4A_0\log(2T+4)\) on \([T,2T]\), and the set of \(\gamma_0\in[T,2T]\) with \(D_W(\gamma_0)\ge m_W(T)/2\) has measure at least \(T\,m_W(T)/(8A_0\log(2T+4))\).

Proof. (1) As \(\varphi\) is integrable and \(G=g\cosh(\cdot/2)\) is continuous and integrable, inversion holds and the previous entry's computation gives \(u(t+iy)=\tfrac1{2\pi}\int\varphi(s)K_y(t-s)\,ds\) (after integrability of \(\varphi\) was established, that proof used nothing else about it). Hence \(u(t+iy)+\eta=\tfrac1{2\pi}\int(\varphi(s)+\eta)K_y(t-s)\,ds\) with \(\varphi+\eta\ge0\), which is nonnegative, and the kernel inequality of the previous entry, integrated against \(\varphi+\eta\), gives the displayed bound. On the boundary \(u=\varphi\ge-\eta\). The hypothesis that \(\varphi\) is integrable is a convenience: the reviewer notes that a bounded harmonic function continuous on the closed strip is the Poisson integral of its boundary values, which I do not prove here. (2) Points of \(Z\) outside the window contribute nonnegatively. For a point inside, by (1), \(u(z_\rho)\ge C_\rho(u_0+\eta)-\eta\) with \(u_0=u(\gamma_0+i\delta)\) and \(C_\rho=\cos(\pi y_\rho)\cos(\pi\delta)e^{-\pi|\gamma_\rho-\gamma_0|}\). So \(B\ge\cos(\pi\delta)D_W(u_0+\eta)-\eta n_W\), and the hypothesis gives \(c\,u_0>\cos(\pi\delta)D_W(u_0+\eta)+\eta(N-n_W)\ge\cos(\pi\delta)D_Wu_0\), using \(\eta\cos(\pi\delta)D_W\ge0\) and \(N\ge n_W\). If \(u_0\le0\), the left side is at most \(0\) while the middle expression is at least \(0\) because \(u_0+\eta\ge0\), which is impossible; so \(u_0>0\) and \(c>\cos(\pi\delta)D_W\). (3) For a point with \(T+1<\gamma_\rho\le2T-1\), the set of \(\gamma_0\in[T,2T]\) with \(|\gamma_\rho-\gamma_0|\le\omega\) is the whole interval of radius \(\omega\le1\), so \(\int_T^{2T}1_{|\gamma_\rho-\gamma_0|\le W}e^{-\pi|\gamma_\rho-\gamma_0|}d\gamma_0\ge\int_{-\omega}^{\omega}e^{-\pi|s|}ds=2(1-e^{-\pi\omega})/\pi\). Summing over the on-line points in that range, for which \(\cos(\pi y_\rho)=1\), \(\int_T^{2T}D_W\ge\tfrac{2(1-e^{-\pi\omega})}{\pi}\kappa N(T+1,2T-1)\), and, as \(T+1\le2T-1\), \(N(T+1,2T-1)=N(T,2T)-N(T,T+1)-N(2T-1,2T)\ge\tfrac T{2\pi}\ell(T)-C\log T-2A_0\log(2T+3)\). For the upper bound, \(D_W\le D\), the sum over all points; the points with \(\gamma_0+k<\gamma_\rho\le\gamma_0+k+1\), \(k\in\mathbb Z\), have \(e^{-\pi|\gamma_\rho-\gamma_0|}\le e^{-\pi j_k}\) with \(j_k=k\) for \(k\ge0\) and \(-k-1\) for \(k<0\), and number at most \(A_0\log(2T+j_k+4)\) for \(\gamma_0\in[T,2T]\); with \(\log(a+j)\le\log a+j\) for \(a\ge1\), \(j\ge0\), the sum is at most \(2A_0\sum_{j\ge0}e^{-\pi j}(\log(2T+4)+j)\le4A_0\log(2T+4)\), since \(\sum_je^{-\pi j}<1.05\) and \(\sum_jje^{-\pi j}<0.05\). Finally, if \(S\) is the set where \(D_W\ge m_W/2\), then \(Tm_W\le\int_T^{2T}D_W\le4A_0\log(2T+4)|S|+Tm_W/2\). \(\square\)

Degenerate cases. With \(\eta=0\), (2) is weaker than the previous lemma's part 3, since only the window's points are counted. A window containing no point of \(Z\) has \(D_W=0\) and (2) is no restriction. \(N\) much larger than \(n_W\) only strengthens the hypothesis. No function \(g\) with \(u\) negative somewhere in the window and nonnegative outside it is exhibited: the nonvacuity of the relaxed class is not shown, and the lemma says only what such functions could not do. In (3), \(\kappa=0\) gives \(m_W=0\); for small \(T\) the quantity \(m_W(T)\) may be negative and the statement is empty; as \(W\to0\), \(m_W\) is about \(2W\kappa\ell(T)/2\pi\), so the lemma gives no obstruction for windows shorter than about \(1/\log T\). The source's \(A_0\ge1\) is not used.

Nonvacuity. Parts 1 and 2: \(g=G/\cosh(\cdot/2)\) with \(G(u)=\max(0,1-|u|/4)\), whose transform \(\varphi(s)=4(\sin(2s)/(2s))^2\) is nonnegative and integrable; \(\eta=0\), \(W=1\), \(\gamma_0=0\), \(\delta=0\), \(Z=\{5\}\) on the real axis, \(B=u(5)\), \(N=0\), \(c=4\): then \(u(0)=\int g>0\) and \(|u(5)|\le u(0)\) because \(g\ge0\), so \(4u(0)>B\), and the conclusion \(D_W=0<4\) holds. This witness has \(\eta=0\); an input with \(u\) actually negative in the window is not exhibited, as said above. Part 3: the multiset of the points \(\gamma_n>2\pi e\) on the real axis defined by \(F(\gamma_n)=n\), \(F(t)=\tfrac t{2\pi}\log\tfrac t{2\pi}-\tfrac t{2\pi}\), has \(N(T,2T)=F(2T)-F(T)+O(1)=\tfrac T{2\pi}\ell(T)+O(1)\) and \(N(t,t+1)\le F'(t+1)+1\), so both count hypotheses hold with \(\kappa=1\).

What it says

The relaxed certificate, in one model. A certificate that allows \(\Re h\ge-\eta\) in a window of half-width \(W\) around \(\gamma_0\), with one \(\eta\) for the whole window, must pay \(\eta\) for every zero that may lie there, and to do so without knowing where they are it uses an upper bound \(N\) for their number; it succeeds if \(4\Re h(\gamma_0+i\delta)>B+\eta N\). Part 2 with \(c=4\): success requires \(\cos(\pi\delta)D_W(\gamma_0)<4\), the condition of the whole-strip form restricted to the zeros of the window. In this model the freedom gained by letting \(\Re h\) be negative is offset by Harnack's inequality applied to \(\Re h+\eta\). Other count-only models are not covered: different depths \(\eta_j\) with counts \(N_j\) in sub-regions, or paying only where \(\Re h\) is actually negative. The reviewer sketches an extension to the first (the same conclusion with the count over the region of deepest negativity), listed below and unverified.

What would escape, as far as this goes. Short windows: for \(W\) below about \(1/\log T\) part 3 gives nothing, and the reviewer's remark that the zeros outside the window then obstruct unless \(\eta\) is large and the count bounded is unverified. Information on the window's zeros beyond one count: a region known to hold few zeros, or zeros confined to a set where \(h\) is small. Whether such a hypothesis can be weaker than a zero-free window is the second half of the sub-idea and is not answered here.

The count, from two statements as given in a hypothesis file. The two hypotheses of part 3 are the fields of the structure named H-RvM in the density project's hypothesis file, where they are assumed, not proved, with the attribution "Titchmarsh, Theorems 9.4 and 9.2"; I read lines 55 to 72 of that file and neither Titchmarsh nor the file's definitions of its counting function and of \(\ell\), which I take to be as written in its docstring. With them and an on-line fraction \(\kappa\) on \((T+1,2T-1]\) (which follows from a fraction \(\kappa'\) on \((T,2T]\) with a loss of \(2A_0\log(2T+3)\) points), \(D_W\ge m_W(T)/2\) on a set of heights of measure at least a constant times \(\kappa(1-e^{-\pi\omega})T\). So, conditionally on \(\kappa>0\) and for a fixed window, at a positive proportion of heights neither form of certificate in the models above can exclude a point with \(\cos(\pi\delta)>8/m_W(T)\). The density import reports a fraction of this kind; I did not re-read its statement and nothing here is based on its value. Without a fraction part 3 is empty.

Sub-ideas

  • Extend part 2 to the general count-only model: depths \(\eta_j\) on regions \(R_j\) of the window with counts \(N_j\); prove or break the reviewer's claim that success forces \(\cos(\pi\delta)D_{R_{\max}}<c\) for the region of deepest negativity, and treat negativity confined to a narrower strip by the rescaled kernel. Exhibit one admissible \(g\) with \(\Re h\) negative in a window and nonnegative outside, or show there is none.
  • Answer the second half of the earlier sub-idea: with \(\Re h\ge0\) on the line in the window and \(\ge-\eta\) off it, state the bound on the number of off-line zeros in the window that a certificate needs, and compare it with a zero-free window (the reviewer expects a bound of order one, a pointwise local density statement).

Follow-up. Continuing: the lead asked whether a positivity certificate at a point can exclude an off-line zero with no hypothesis on the others. Proved so far: with positivity on the whole strip, or relaxed to \(-\eta\) in one window with one count, success requires \(\cos(\pi\delta)D_W(\gamma_0)<4\). I drafted a closure on this and the review did not uphold it: the reason given needed the count of part 3 while saying it did not, other count-only models are open, and half of the sub-idea is unanswered. The lead continues with the two sub-ideas above. This is its second entry in a row declaring no bearing, so the next entry develops a different item. Record search. For the sub-ideas the registry was searched for relaxed positivity window local zero density certificate, and for positive proportion heights weighted zero count average Riemann von Mangoldt local count: no record on these objects. The lead's earlier sub-idea:
